➡️ Recommendation: put withdrawals in a new hexagon (`app/withdrawals/{domain,application,adapters}`), registered in `HEXAGON_ROOTS`. Keep the draw's cost and tie order in `app/stock`, which needs a `WithdrawalDraw` shape and a `WITHDRAWAL_RANK`. Turn the ternary in `build_draw_rows` into an exhaustive `match` with `assert_never`. Distinguish the kind by an explicit field on `Draw`, such as `source` or `reason`, not by absent `sale_id`. Write ADR-0030 amending ADR-0010 with the tie rule (negative adjustment, then withdrawal, then sale line on an exact tie, or whatever the Tech Lead decides). Without it, the Inicio and Análisis agreement test cannot pin the number.

❓ **Q2** - **One period computation and the pending marks**: `period_figures.py` is 575 lines with one `scan`, `Buckets` and profit formula (ADR-0013 amendment, ADR-0020). Ganancia must subtract both withdrawal costs, and each reason has its own pending mark. Profit's pending marker must also include withdrawn pending grams. Variety margins, best week, best weekday, cost of goods sold and shortfall must not move. How do you extend `Buckets` and `PeriodTotals` without a second summation path? How do you stop pending from being $0 or double counted, given ADR-0010's invariant that total pending equals `max(0, -stock)`? What prevents a field added to `Buckets` from leaking into a ranking that sums every bucket field?
➡️ Recommendation: add the two costs and two pending counts as named fields in `Buckets` and `PeriodTotals`, and derive profit in the one existing formula only. Add a domain test where the same withdrawal appears in Semana, Mes and Análisis and the totals agree. Add a test where a withdrawal alone makes pending grams, and profit shows a pending mark but costs of goods sold show none. Add a property test that withdrawals leave margins, best week and best weekday unchanged.

❓ **Q3** - **Persistence shape and the business boundary**: A withdrawal has a header (reason, date, note) and one or more lines (variety, grams). That means two tables, both needing `business_id` (ADR-0004), a repository per business, 404 for another business's id, and the isolation sweep and the ownership migration test updated. ADR-0015 needs a declared label for the lines table, which has no `name`. CODING_STANDARDS §11 requires `withdrawn_on` for the date, `recorded_at` for the instant, `Index(None, ...)` with `op.f`, and no `name=`. §9 forbids note and grams in logs and errors. Which of these does the plan cover? Where do the refusal codes (invalid reason, future date, note over 200 characters, no lines) go in `contract.json` (ADR-0008)? What happens to an edit that replaces lines: delete-and-reinsert changes `LotOrder.position`, and so changes the allocation of every later draw.
➡️ Recommendation: give lines a stable `position` assigned at creation. On edit, update in place and replace the set inside one transaction. Put the `business_id` on the lines table too, not only on the header, so the sweep finds it. Write one migration test that covers both tables. Make the edit and delete paths trigger the same after-commit upkeep as creation (ADR-0014).

❓ **Q4** - **Test seams**: Which tests protect what? My proposal: pure domain tests for `allocate` and `lots_and_draws` with three sources and the tie order. Behaviour tests through `TestClient` for create, edit and delete, including "never refused for lack of stock" and "sales count, Ventas, customers and Caja unchanged". An agreement test where Inicio (`GET /panel`) and Análisis (`GET /analysis`) return the same Promoción and Consumo propio figures. The architecture test that sales and payments do not import withdrawals. What does the plan propose instead, and where would a mutation (leaving the kind in the default branch) be caught?
➡️ Recommendation: keep all four. Add a regression test that creates a withdrawal larger than stock and asserts the shortfall and cost of goods sold do not change, and that the withdrawal's own pending is non-zero. That one test catches the Q1 ternary failure directly.

❓ **Q5** - **Splitting into subtasks**: My sketch of the pieces is below. Which of them carries more than about four production modules plus its tests plus an ADR and needs its own seam?
 1. Stock domain (`Draw` source, `WITHDRAWAL_RANK`, `lots_and_draws`, `allocate` tie, ADR-0030) plus the `build_draw_rows` match.
 2. The `app/withdrawals` hexagon: domain, application, router, tables and migration, `contract.json` codes, business removal label.
 3. Results: `DrawKind`, `Buckets`, `PeriodTotals`, panel and analysis schemas.
 4. Front: sheet via `useSheet` (ADR-0027), Inicio and Análisis lines (blocked on #59?).
 What runs in parallel, and what is the contract between 1 and 3 (the `DrawRow` kinds)? Piece 3 touches the 575-line `period_figures.py` and the schemas, so it looks like the one most at risk of exceeding the cap.
➡️ Recommendation: freeze the `DrawRow` kinds and the `Draw` source in a first small subtask (piece 1 plus the `DrawKind` literal). Then run pieces 2 and 3 in parallel against it. Split piece 3 into "period figures domain" and "panel and analysis schemas and wiring". The front starts only once the schema is fixed.

### Invariants proposed
- Every `match` or branch over `DrawKind` is exhaustive with no default branch, so a new kind cannot fall into `shortfall` or `sold`.
- A withdrawal draw is identified by an explicit source or reason field on `Draw`, never by the absence of `sale_id` or of a pricing rule.
- No profit, cost or pending figure is summed outside `app/results/domain/period_figures.py`.
- Every new table carries a non-null `business_id`, a declared business-removal label if it has no `name`, and §11 names for its date, instant and indexes.
- No module under `app/sales` or `app/payments` imports a withdrawals module.

### Round 1: answers

### Answers, round 1

I read `app/stock/domain/{lot_order,allocation,stock_level}.py`, `app/stock/adapters/{draw_rows,sql_draws,tables,router}.py`, `app/results/domain/{ledger,period_figures,figures}.py`, `app/business_removal/domain/labels.py` and the wiring in `app/main.py`. Two things from that reading change the picture:

- **Five callers need the new draws.** `lots_and_draws` has five callers: `read_stock` (twice), `SqlLineCosts`, `SqlDrawCosts` and `SqlStockFigures`. If any of them misses the withdrawals, a sale's line cost in `GET /sales/{id}` stops matching results, or stock stops matching cost. So it is not only `build_draw_rows`.
- **Days left counts sale lines only today.** `stock_level._days_left` counts only draws with `rank == SALE_LINE_RANK`. Withdrawals would be left out of days left.

**Q1**: **Overruled on the hexagon; accepted on the rest.** Withdrawals live in `app/stock/`, not in a new hexagon. The reasons, from the code and the glossary:

- **It is stock's language.** stock.md defines the withdrawal, and CONTEXT.md makes it a Draw.
- **It is stock's data.** Its only behaviour is to take grams off lots, and lots, `LotOrder`, `allocate` and `stock_level` are all stock's domain.
- **Upkeep comes for free.** `stock_router` is already included with `upkeep_gated`, so `/stock/withdrawals` gets ADR-0014's after-commit trigger with no new wiring.
- **A new hexagon would cost more.** Stock would need a port to read a hexagon whose only consumer is stock, plus a second composition in `app/main.py`, for no seam anyone needs.

Nothing outside stock reads withdrawals except results, through the existing `DrawCosts` and `StockFigures` rows. The no-cross-hexagon-import rule already keeps sales and payments from importing stock.

The draw contract, frozen in subtask 1:

- **The source is explicit.** `Draw` gains a required `source: DrawSource`, a closed enum in `app/stock/domain/lot_order.py`: `ADJUSTMENT`, `SALE_LINE`, `PROMOTION`, `OWN_USE`. No code tells a draw's kind by a missing `sale_id` or pricing rule any more. Promotion and own use sit directly in `DrawSource`, so every `match` stays flat and exhaustive. The withdrawal's own `WithdrawalReason` enum, in `domain/withdrawal.py`, maps onto `DrawSource` in one exhaustive function.
- **The withdrawal line's shape.** `WithdrawalLineDraw(variety_id, day, recorded_at, withdrawal_id, position, grams, reason)` sits next to `SaleLineDraw`, with `WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK = 2`. `STOCK_ENTRY_RANK = 0` and `SALE_LINE_RANK = 1` keep their values, so every existing order is unchanged.
- **The tie rule (ADR-0030, amending ADR-0010's `LotOrder`).** Lot order is `(day, recorded_at, rank, ref_id, position)`. On an exact tie of day and instant, a negative adjustment goes first, then a sale line, then a withdrawal line. A withdrawal line's `ref_id` is the withdrawal's id and its `position` the line's position. Exact instant ties don't happen in practice, since `recorded_at` comes from the clock in µs. The rule exists so the order is total and the tests can pin it.
- **Every caller must pass withdrawals.** `lots_and_draws(entries, sale_draws, withdrawal_draws)` takes the third argument with no default, so `mypy` fails any of the five callers that forgets it.
- **`build_draw_rows`** becomes `match draw.source` with `assert_never`. `SALE_LINE` splits into gifted or sold by pricing rule, as today. `ADJUSTMENT` → `"shortfall"`, `PROMOTION` → `"promotion"`, `OWN_USE` → `"own_use"`. A withdrawal row carries `variety_id=None`, as a shortfall row does: there is no breakdown per variety (out of scope).
- **`DrawKind`** becomes `Literal["sold", "gifted", "shortfall", "promotion", "own_use"]`.
- **`_days_left`** counts by an exhaustive `match` on source: sale lines and both withdrawal sources count, adjustments do not. That is CONTEXT.md's "sold, gifted or withdrawn".
- **Already right once withdrawals are draws:** "no entries" (`not lots and not draws`) and "Sin gramos desde" (every draw already counts).

**Q2**: **Accepted, with this shape.**

- **Buckets.** `Buckets` gains `withdrawal_totals: dict[tuple[date, DrawSource-like reason], _CostDayTotals]`. `_CostDayTotals` (costed, pending_grams) is the same shape as `_ShortfallDayTotals`, which it replaces and generalises. One private helper, `_cost_line(totals, start, end) -> AmountWithPending | None`, answers `shortfall`, `promotion` and `own_use`: `None` exactly when no draw of that kind falls in the window.
- **`scan`** turns into `match row.kind` with `assert_never`. `"promotion"` and `"own_use"` write only into `withdrawal_totals`. They never touch `day_totals`, `variety_day_totals`, `sales_by_day` or `weekday_*`.
- **Margins can't see withdrawals.** Margins, variety rows, grams sold or gifted, best week (sales only) and best weekday (sales only) read only those untouched dicts. No ranking sums every bucket field, so nothing can leak.
- **Profit stays one formula.** `Buckets.profit` subtracts the costed amount of promotion and own use, and adds their pending grams to profit's pending, in the one existing formula.
- **What the answers carry:**
  - `PeriodTotals`, `MonthFigures`, `WeekFigures`, `MonthRowFigures` and the since-first-record totals gain `promotion` and `own_use`, each `AmountWithPending | None`.
  - The panel answers `promotion` and `own_use` as `{amount, pending} | null`. `ShortfallOut` is generalised to `CostLineOut`, with no grams on the wire, as for shortfall.
  - `profit_pending` comes from profit's own pending grams.
- **No double count, no $0.** Each draw lands in exactly one kind branch, and its `pending` comes from `allocate` as `Grams`, never as a cost. Every draw, withdrawals included, comes from the one loader feeding `allocate` and `stock_level`, so ADR-0010's "total pending = max(0, −stock)" holds unchanged. A domain test asserts it with a withdrawal past zero.
- **Tests: your three are accepted.**
  - Agreement across Semana, Mes and Análisis.
  - A withdrawal alone giving profit a pending mark and the cost of goods none.
  - A property test that withdrawals leave margins, best week and best weekday unchanged.
- **One point is open, sent to PM:** whether a withdrawal counts as a first record. Read as written, the glossary's list leaves it out, and that can drop a withdrawal's cost from Análisis's history.

**Q3**: **Partly accepted.**

- **Tables, in migration `0012_stock_withdrawals`:**
  - `stock_withdrawal`: `id`, `business_id`, `reason` (String), `withdrawn_on` (date), `note` (nullable, 200), `recorded_at` (`UtcDateTime`, set at creation and never changed by an edit, like `stock_entry`).
  - `stock_withdrawal_line`: `id`, `business_id`, `stock_withdrawal_id`, `variety_id`, `position`, `grams_hundredths`, with `UniqueConstraint("stock_withdrawal_id", "position")` and `Index(None, "business_id", "variety_id")`.
  - All names come from `NAMING_CONVENTION`, spelled with `op.f(...)` in the migration, with no `name=`. Both tables carry a non-null `business_id`.
- **Business removal:** labels in `labels.py`. `LABEL_COLUMN["stock_withdrawal"] = "withdrawn_on"` and `LABEL_VIA["stock_withdrawal_line"] = "variety_id"`. Reflection exports and deletes both tables.
- **The repository** `SqlStockWithdrawals(session, business_id)` filters every statement, the `ON` clause of the line join included. Another business's withdrawal answers 404.
- **Variety check:** each line's variety is checked through stock's existing `VarietyCatalog.exists`. A foreign or unknown variety is gone (404).
- **Edit: overruled.** On edit, the header row keeps its `id` and `recorded_at`, and the lines are deleted and inserted again with positions `0..n-1` in the order sent, in the use case's one commit. Your worry doesn't hold:
  - `position` only breaks ties inside the same withdrawal, behind `(day, recorded_at, rank, ref_id)`, which the edit doesn't change.
  - So renumbering never moves any other draw's place in lot order. The later draws' costs move only because the grams or the date changed, which is exactly what ADR-0009 asks for.
  - Updating lines in place would match lines across two different sets for nothing.
  - Line ids are never exposed; an edit sends the whole withdrawal.
- **Refusal codes.** Each lives on a domain error class with `code: ClassVar[str]` and goes in `contract.json` (ADR-0008):

  | Code | Kind | Raised when |
  |---|---|---|
  | `stock.withdrawal_reason_required` | refusal | the reason is missing or not one of the two |
  | `stock.withdrawal_date_in_future` | refusal | the date is after today |
  | `stock.withdrawal_no_lines` | refusal | there are no lines |
  | `stock.withdrawal_grams_not_positive` | refusal | grams at or below zero |
  | `stock.withdrawal_grams_too_precise` | refusal | grams with more than two decimals |
  | `stock.withdrawal_note_too_long` | refusal | the note is over 200 characters |
  | `stock.withdrawal_not_found` | gone | the withdrawal does not exist in the business |
  | `stock.withdrawal_variety_not_found` | gone | a line's variety does not exist in the business |

- **Schemas stay bare** (ADR-0005). `reason: str | None`, so the domain decides, never pydantic.
- **Fixed messages only.** No note, grams or amount appears in any message.
- **Upkeep:** create, edit and delete all go through `stock_router`, so the after-commit upkeep is the same as for entries.
- **The variety page's history** reads a new `GET /stock/varieties/{variety_id}/movements`. It answers entries and withdrawals, each tagged `kind: "entry" | "withdrawal"`, merged and ordered on the server by (day desc, recorded_at desc, kind, id desc). For a withdrawal row it answers that variety's grams in it. "Todas las entradas" stays entries only.
- **Isolation sweep:** the seeder records a withdrawal on the `MARKER` variety, and every new route has a body in `BODIES`.

**Q4**: **Accepted: all four seams, your regression test, and two more.**

- **Your regression test.** A withdrawal past stock leaves shortfall and cost of goods sold unchanged, and carries its own non-zero pending. It catches a withdrawal falling into `"shortfall"`.
- **A sale's line cost through HTTP.** `GET /sales/{id}` shows a line's cost moving when an earlier-dated withdrawal is recorded, edited and deleted. It catches a `lots_and_draws` caller fed without withdrawals. `mypy` catches the same thing statically, through the required argument.
- **Days left and status through `GET /stock`.**
  - Withdrawn grams pace days left.
  - A variety with only a withdrawal is sold out, not "sin entradas".
  - "Sin gramos desde" follows the withdrawal's date.

A mutation that sends a kind into the wrong branch is caught three ways:

- `mypy` (`assert_never`) catches a missing branch.
- The regression test catches a wrong mapping.
- The agreement test (`GET /panel` against `GET /analysis`, seeded per the PRD's metric) catches an unequal sum.

The upkeep trigger test goes in pinned-catalog's trigger matrix: a withdrawal that takes a variety to zero edits the pin, and one that leaves the text unchanged sends nothing.

**Q5**: **Accepted, re-cut to match Q1.**

1. **Contract (stock domain).** `domain/withdrawal.py` (aggregate, reason, errors), `lot_order.py` (`DrawSource`, `WithdrawalLineDraw`, rank, three-argument `lots_and_draws`), `stock_level.py` (days left), and the `Withdrawals` and `WithdrawalDraws` ports. Plus `results/domain/ledger.py`'s `DrawKind` literal, and ADR-0030. Domain tests only.
2. **After 1, in parallel:**
   - **2a, persistence.** Tables, migration 0012 and its ownership and naming tests, `SqlStockWithdrawals`, the five `lots_and_draws` callers fed from the port, and the removal labels.
   - **3a, results domain.** The `draw_rows.py` match, `period_figures.py` (`Buckets`, `scan`, `PeriodTotals`) and `figures.py`, with domain tests over `DrawRow` fixtures.
3. **Then:**
   - **2b (after 2a).** `application/manage_withdrawals.py`, router and schemas, the movements read, `contract.json`, the sweep and the gated routes, and the HTTP behaviour tests (Q4's first two).
   - **3b (after 3a and 2a).** Results' `schemas.py` and `assemble.py`, the panel and analysis answers, and the agreement test through HTTP.
4. **Front, once 2b and 3b have fixed the schemas** and #59 is on master. Front routes `/withdrawals/new` and `/withdrawals/:id`: the first segment `withdrawals` is not an API segment, so ADR-0024 isn't needed, and the exact paths go in `screens.json`. The form is a route (a form under the navigation rule), not a sheet; any sheet inside it (picking a variety) uses `useSheet`. Phone inputs follow #80. Más, Variedades and the variety's page get their entries; Inicio and Análisis get the lines.

`period_figures.py` grows only by the helper and the match branches, so 3a stays within the cap.

### On the invariants proposed
- **Accepted.** Every branch over `DrawKind` or `DrawSource` is an exhaustive `match` with `assert_never`.
- **Accepted.** A draw's kind comes from `Draw.source`, never from a missing field.
- **Accepted.** Profit, cost and pending are summed only in `period_figures.py`.
- **Accepted.** Every new table has a non-null `business_id`, a declared label, and §11 names (`withdrawn_on`, `recorded_at`, convention-made index names).
- **Restated.** No module outside `app/stock` and the results ports' stock adapters reads the withdrawal tables. Sales and payments are already kept out by the cross-hexagon rule.
- **Added.** Every caller of `lots_and_draws` passes withdrawal draws; the argument has no default.
- **Added.** `STOCK_ENTRY_RANK` and `SALE_LINE_RANK` keep their values (ADR-0030).
- **Coexistence.** `stock_entry.entry_date` keeps its allowlisted name next to the new `withdrawn_on`, and `_ShortfallDayTotals` is generalised, not duplicated.

### Round 1: PM answers

### Answers

**Q1. Is a stock withdrawal one of a business's first records?** Yes. A withdrawal counts as a first record.

The job is that Ganancia shows what promoting the business and my own consumption cost me. A withdrawal dated before every other record would lower Inicio's Ganancia for its week or month and still be missing from Análisis's history and its since-the-first-record totals. That breaks the PRD's criterion 5 (one computation) and Análisis's criterion 2.

What this changes:
- **First record** (glossary) is now the earliest sale, stock entry, stock withdrawal, payment or expense the business recorded in the app.
- **Análisis's history starts there.** A business whose only record is a withdrawal has a history: the withdrawal's week and month show $0 sales and their Promoción or Consumo propio line, and profit goes below zero by that cost.
- **"Nothing to analyse yet"** shows only when the business has no record of any of the five kinds.
- **A new metric in analysis.md**: a withdrawal dated before every other record starts the history. Its cost is in that week's and month's rows and in the since-the-first-record profit. Inicio and Análisis still agree with 0 differences.
- **Nothing else moves.** "Best weekday" still counts sales only. A negative adjustment is already a stock entry, so it is already covered.

This adds to the approved PRD without reopening anything. The PRD's "Results › Análisis" now also reads: "A withdrawal counts as a first record: Análisis's history and its since-the-first-record totals begin at the earliest sale, stock entry, stock withdrawal, payment or expense."

### Round 2: questions

### Round 2

Failures I can name against the plan as answered:

- **First record has no home yet.** `scan` in `period_figures.py` folds sales, payments and expenses into `first_record` (lines 273, 279, 294). Stock entries arrive through a separate port method, `purchases.earliest_entry_day()`, called in both `read_panel.py:94` and `analyse.py:66`. A withdrawal dated before every other record is in none of those, so PM's new rule would fail exactly where PM said it would.
- **The assembly is repeated at five call sites.** A `lots_and_draws` signature with no default makes `mypy` catch a missing argument. It does not catch a caller that passes withdrawals unscoped. `read_stock.py:67` and `line_costs.py:31` call `.for_varieties(...)` on both entries and draws, so a withdrawal reader that ignores that narrowing quietly differs between `GET /stock` and `GET /sales/{id}`.
- **A unique constraint can trip on edit.** `UniqueConstraint("stock_withdrawal_id", "position")` plus "delete the lines and insert them again with positions 0..n-1 in one commit" works only if the delete reaches the database before the insert. A SQLAlchemy ORM unit of work flushes INSERTs before DELETEs, so an edit that keeps a line count would raise an integrity error and surface as a 500.

❓ **Q1** - **Where a withdrawal's date reaches `first_record`**: PM settled that a withdrawal is a first record, and Análisis's history and since-first-record totals start there. Today the stock entry day comes from the `earliest_entry_day` port, and sales, payments and expenses come from `scan`'s own pass. Does the plan add a third path (a new port method, for example `earliest_withdrawal_day`), or does `scan` fold the day of the `"promotion"` and `"own_use"` rows it already visits in its `match row.kind`? Does Inicio's panel path also receive these draw rows? If `SqlStockFigures.read` gives the panel only aggregates, then `GET /panel` and `GET /analysis` would disagree on the first record.
➡️ Recommendation: fold it in `scan`'s `match` branches for `"promotion"` and `"own_use"`, in the one place that already owns `first_record`. Add no port method. Add a test in which a withdrawal is the only record: Análisis's history starts on its week and month with $0 sales and a negative profit, and "nothing to analyse yet" is absent. Add a second test that a withdrawal dated before every other record moves `first_record` in both `GET /panel` and `GET /analysis`.

❓ **Q2** - **One assembly of lots and draws instead of five**: `lots_and_draws(entries, sale_draws, withdrawal_draws)` is called from `read_stock` (twice), `SqlLineCosts`, `SqlDrawCosts` and `SqlStockFigures`. Each one fetches entries and sale draws on its own, and some narrow by variety. The plan adds a third fetch to each. The next draw source would need a sixth edit in five places, and the narrowing could differ by call site.
➡️ Recommendation: add one function in `app/stock/application` (or the adapter layer) that takes a `for_varieties` selection, `None` meaning all, and returns the `lots_and_draws` grouping for exactly those varieties from all three sources. The five callers call only that. `lots_and_draws` stays pure. A test feeds the same fixtures through two callers (one narrowed, one not) and asserts the same costs for the same variety.

❓ **Q3** - **Edit, duplicates and the variety's own lifecycle**: Three gaps in the persistence answer.
(a) The unique constraint and the delete-then-insert order, as in the failure above. Is the line replacement a Core `DELETE` flushed before the inserts, or an ORM relationship?
(b) Can the same variety appear in two lines of one withdrawal? The PRD says "one or more variety+grams lines" and nothing about uniqueness. The movements read, which answers "that variety's grams in it", has to either sum them or list two rows, so the plan must pick one. If PM must rule on this, say so.
(c) What happens to withdrawal lines when a variety is deleted or archived? Sale lines and stock entries have some rule today. If the new FK has none, deleting a variety with only a withdrawal line either fails with a 500 or orphans the line and changes every cost computed from it.
➡️ Recommendation: (a) delete with an explicit statement and `flush()` it before the inserts, and add an HTTP edit test with an unchanged line count. (b) Refuse a repeated variety within one withdrawal with a refusal code such as `stock.withdrawal_variety_repeated`, after consulting PM, or sum the lines on write. Do not keep both shapes. (c) Mirror what stock entries do, and test it through the variety delete route.

❓ **Q4** - **Subtask 1 and subtask 2a are over the cap**: By my count, subtask 1 carries `domain/withdrawal.py`, `lot_order.py`, `stock_level.py`, the two ports and `results/domain/ledger.py`, which is five or six production modules plus ADR-0030 plus its tests. Subtask 2a carries `tables.py`, migration 0012, `SqlStockWithdrawals`, the five-caller change (now the Q2 function), `labels.py` and the ownership and naming tests. Subtask 3a also grows now: `draw_rows.py`, `period_figures.py` with `first_record`, `figures.py`, and the Análisis "nothing to analyse yet" rule. Which of these needs its own seam?
➡️ Recommendation: split 1 into 1a (`DrawSource`, `WithdrawalLineDraw`, rank, three-argument `lots_and_draws`, `stock_level` days left, `DrawKind` literal, ADR-0030) and 1b (the `Withdrawal` aggregate, reasons and error classes with `contract.json` codes). 1b depends on nothing in 1a, so they can run in parallel. Split 2a into "tables, migration and labels" and "the repository, the Q2 assembly function and the callers". Keep 3a together only if `first_record` stays inside `scan` (Q1).

### Invariants proposed
- `first_record` is computed only inside `scan` in `period_figures.py`, with no per-source port method that duplicates a date `scan` already visits.
- Every read that feeds `allocate` or `stock_level` gets its lots and draws from the one assembly function, never from a direct call to `lots_and_draws` with its own entry and draw fetches.
- Replacing a withdrawal's lines runs the delete before the inserts, so the `(stock_withdrawal_id, position)` constraint cannot fail on an edit.

### Round 2: answers

### Answers, round 2

PM has ruled that a withdrawal counts as a first record. That now holds as part of the PRD.

**Q1**: **Accepted.** `scan` folds a withdrawal's day into `first_record`, in the `"promotion"` and `"own_use"` branches of its `match row.kind`. No new port method.

- **Both routes get it.** I checked that both routes already give `scan` the same draw rows: `read_panel.py:88` passes `stock_answer.draws` (from `SqlStockFigures.read`, which calls `build_draw_rows`), and `analyse.py:61` passes `draw_costs.rows()`. So a withdrawal row reaches `scan` on both, and `GET /panel` and `GET /analysis` see the same `first_record`.
- **Your two tests are accepted:**
  - A withdrawal as the only record: the history starts on its week and month, with $0 sales and negative profit, and "nothing to analyse yet" is absent.
  - A withdrawal dated before every other record moves `first_record` in both routes. The agreement holds with 0 differences.
- **`earliest_entry_day()` stays.** A positive stock entry is a lot, not a draw, so it never reaches `scan` as a row. That port is the only way a lot's date gets in, so it is not a duplicate. A negative adjustment's day stays covered twice (as an entry and as a `"shortfall"` row), which is harmless for a minimum. `scan` doesn't fold shortfall rows today and doesn't need to.

**Q2**: **Accepted.**

- **One function.** `app/stock/application/history.py::history(entries, draws, withdrawals, variety_ids: Collection[int] | None)` takes stock's three ports (`StockEntries`, `Draws`, `WithdrawalDraws`). It reads all three sources with the same `variety_ids` (`None` means every variety) and returns `lots_and_draws(...)`.
- **All five callers go through it:** both `read_stock` functions, `costs_for_sale` in `SqlLineCosts`, `SqlDrawCosts.rows` and `SqlStockFigures.read`. None of them calls `lots_and_draws` or a source's `for_varieties` itself any more.
- **`lots_and_draws` stays pure** and keeps its required third argument.
- **The next draw source** is one edit in `history`.
- **Your test is accepted.** The same fixtures go through `GET /sales/{id}` (narrowed) and `GET /analysis` (not narrowed), with an earlier withdrawal on that sale's variety and another variety alongside it. The sale line's costed amount and pending grams are the same both ways.

**Q3**: Taken in parts.

- **(a) Accepted.**
  - `SqlStockWithdrawals.replace` follows the shape `SqlSales.delete` already has: a Core `sa_delete(StockWithdrawalLineRow).where(business_id, stock_withdrawal_id)` run through `session.execute` before any line is added.
  - Before that statement, autoflush can only write the header's update, never a line. Then the update and the new lines are added and flushed. There is no ORM relationship between the header and its lines.
  - HTTP tests: an edit with the same number of lines, and one that reverses their order, each answer 200 with the new lines. A 500 cannot happen.
- **(b) Settled by the definition, no PM needed.** stock.md says "The same variety may appear in several lines, with no merging", the same rule a sale line has. So there is no repeated-variety refusal, and lines are never merged on write.
  - The withdrawal's detail lists every line as recorded.
  - `GET /stock/varieties/{variety_id}/movements` answers one row per withdrawal touching that variety, with grams equal to the sum of that variety's lines in it. That is the PRD's "that variety's grams in the withdrawal", one row per movement.
  - Lot order still takes each line as its own draw (by `position`).
- **(c) Nothing to mirror.** No route deletes a variety: varieties.md lists "Deleting a variety" as out of scope, and `app/varieties/adapters/router.py` only deletes price entries.
  - `stock_withdrawal_line.variety_id` gets a plain foreign key, with no cascade, as `sale_line` and `stock_entry` have.
  - The only path that deletes a variety is business removal. It deletes in reverse foreign-key order, read from the schema (ADR-0015).
  - Its existing "nothing survives" test, seeded with a withdrawal of two lines, covers both new tables.
  - Whoever later adds a variety delete decides that rule for sale lines, entries and withdrawal lines together.

**Q4**: **Accepted, with one adjustment to the order.**

- **1a**: `DrawSource`, `WithdrawalLineDraw`, `WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK`, the three-argument `lots_and_draws`, days left in `stock_level`, the `DrawKind` literal, and ADR-0030.
- **1b**, in parallel with 1a: the `Withdrawal` aggregate in `domain/withdrawal.py`, its reasons and its error classes with codes, plus the `contract.json` codes and their check in `tests/test_refusal_codes.py`.
  - The codes join `contract.json` only together with the router that registers them (2b), since `test_refusal_codes.py` checks registered classes against the JSON.
  - So 1b writes the classes and their domain tests, and 2b adds the JSON entries and registers the classes in the same diff.
- **2a-i**, after 1b: tables, migration 0012, the business-removal labels, and the ownership, naming and schema tests.
- **2a-ii**, after 1a and 2a-i: `SqlStockWithdrawals`, the `WithdrawalDraws` port's SQL, `history.py` and the five callers. It ends with the Q2 test through the sale detail once 2b exists, or a repository-level version of it before then.
- **3a**, after 1a, stays whole, since `first_record` stays inside `scan`: `draw_rows.py`, `period_figures.py`, `figures.py` and the "nothing to analyse" rule, which follows from `first_record` with no new code.
- **2b and 3b** as before. Then the front.

### On the invariants proposed
- **Accepted, restated.** `first_record` is folded only inside `scan`. The one outside input is `earliest_entry_day`, because lots never reach `scan` as rows, and it stays that way.
- **Accepted.** Every read feeding `allocate` or `stock_level` goes through `history()`, and `lots_and_draws` is called only from there.
- **Accepted.** Replacing a withdrawal's lines runs a Core delete through `session.execute` before any line is added, so `uq_stock_withdrawal_line_stock_withdrawal_id_position` cannot fail on an edit.

### Round 3: questions

### Round 3

I wrote `docs/adr/0030-a-withdrawal-is-a-third-draw-source-in-stock-with-an-explicit-source.md` from your round 1 and 2 answers (the hexagon placement, `DrawSource`, the rank and tie order, `history()`, `DrawKind`). So subtask 1a no longer writes ADR-0030; it only has to match it. The glossary already carries Stock withdrawal and First record, so I wrote nothing there.

Failures I can still name:

- **Upkeep after an edit or delete.** An edit can move a withdrawal from variety A to variety B, or drop a line. The after-commit upkeep (ADR-0014) works from the varieties whose stock text can change. If it looks only at the varieties of the lines as they are after the edit, variety A's pin goes stale. On delete there are no "new lines" at all.
- **A stored reason with no guard.** `reason` is a plain `String` column. A value the code does not know (a hand-edited row, a later rename) makes `WithdrawalReason(row.reason)` raise on read. That 500 would break `GET /panel` for the whole business, because every withdrawal is read to cost every draw.
- **Subtask 4 (front) is the biggest piece and has no seam yet.** It covers the form, the detail and edit, delete, Inicio lines, Análisis lines, and the entries in Más, Variedades and the variety's page.

❓ **Q1** - **Upkeep over the union of the varieties before and after**: How does the create, edit and delete use case tell the after-commit trigger which varieties changed? For an edit that changes a line's variety or date, and for a delete, which varieties are passed?
➡️ Recommendation: the use case loads the withdrawal before it changes it. It returns the union of the varieties in the old lines and in the new ones, and the trigger gets that set after commit. Add three tests in the pinned-catalog trigger matrix: an edit moving a line from A to B updates both pins, a delete updates the pin of every variety it had, and an edit that changes only the note sends nothing because the text did not change.

❓ **Q2** - **How the reason is stored and read**: Which values go in the `reason` column (the `WithdrawalReason` values, or the Spanish labels)? Is there a `CheckConstraint` (§11 naming, by convention, no `name=`)? What does a read do with an unknown value? Where does the one exhaustive function mapping `WithdrawalReason` to `DrawSource` live, and who else may call it?
➡️ Recommendation: store the enum's value in English (`promotion`, `own_use`). Add a `CheckConstraint` with the two values, so the database refuses anything else, and make the read build `WithdrawalReason(...)` in one place. Keep the mapping function in `domain/withdrawal.py` and have only the `WithdrawalDraws` adapter call it. A schema test checks that the constraint lists exactly the enum's values.

❓ **Q3** - **Clock seams for "not future" and for ordering**: The date check needs "today" and `recorded_at` needs an instant. Do both come from the same injected clock that stock entries use, in the business's day (not the server's UTC date)? How do the tests set up the tie order you pinned in ADR-0030 (a withdrawal and a sale at the same day and instant), given that exact µs ties never happen in practice?
➡️ Recommendation: use the existing injected clock for both, and test "today" at a boundary where the UTC date and the business date differ. Pin the tie order with pure domain tests on `lots_and_draws` built from fixtures with equal `day` and `recorded_at`, not through HTTP.

❓ **Q4** - **Front: split and contract**: Subtask 4 holds `/withdrawals/new` and `/withdrawals/:id` (create, edit, delete), the reason choice with no default, the variety and grams lines (repeated varieties allowed), Inicio lines and pending marks, Análisis lines in the weekly and monthly rows and in the since-first-record totals, the `screens.json` entries, and the Más, Variedades and variety-page entries with their movements list. Is it one subtask? Where do the front's wire types come from: generated from the API schema, or written by hand? If by hand, what proves they match `CostLineOut` and the movements row?
➡️ Recommendation: split it into 4a (the form route: create, edit, delete, `screens.json`, entries in Más and Variedades) and 4b (the Inicio and Análisis lines, and the variety page's movements). Run 4a and 4b in parallel after 2b and 3b. Put the wire types in one shared front module that both import, and cover them with a contract check against the backend's schema if one exists.

### Invariants proposed
- A use case that edits or deletes a withdrawal passes the after-commit upkeep the union of the varieties of its lines before and after the change.
- A withdrawal's `reason` is stored and read only as a `WithdrawalReason` value, and the column has a `CheckConstraint` listing exactly those values.

### Round 3: answers

### Answers, round 3

ADR-0030 matches what I decided. Subtask 1a builds to it and doesn't rewrite it.

**Q1**: **Overruled. No set of varieties is passed, because upkeep takes none.**

- **How upkeep works today.** `app/pinned_catalog/adapters/upkeep_trigger.py::schedule_upkeep` is a router dependency. It queues `run_upkeep_safely(app.state, business_id, today)` after any successful `POST`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE`. `push` then rebuilds the business's whole catalog text and edits the pin only when it differs from the last published text (ADR-0014, `needs_send`).
- **Why A can't go stale.** The use case never reports varieties, so it can't report the wrong ones: the text is rebuilt for every variety, A included. A delete is a `DELETE` on `stock_router`, which is already included with `upkeep_gated`, so it triggers too.
- **Why a set would be worse.** Adding a set of varieties would be a second, narrower author of "what may have changed", and that is the very failure you describe.
- **Your three trigger-matrix tests are accepted**, against that per-business mechanism. Each runs through HTTP with the fake Telegram adapter, and A and B are the only shown, priced varieties:
  1. An edit that moves a withdrawal's only line from A to B, taking A to zero and B from zero to below zero, edits the pin once, and the new text lacks A.
  2. A delete that brings a variety back above zero edits the pin, and the text has it again.
  3. An edit of the note only sends nothing.
- **The invariant becomes:** every withdrawal write goes through `stock_router`, which is included with `upkeep_gated`. A test asserts that every `/stock/withdrawals` write route carries `schedule_upkeep`, the same way the existing trigger list is tested.

**Q2**: **Accepted, with two adjustments.**

- **Stored values.** The column stores `WithdrawalReason`'s English values, `promotion` and `own_use`, the same way `stock_entry.origin` stores `Origin`'s values. Spanish lives only in the front.
- **Check constraint.** `stock_withdrawal` gets `CheckConstraint("reason IN ('promotion', 'own_use')", "reason")`: the short name only, with no `name=`, so the convention makes it `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason`. The migration spells it with `op.f(...)`.
  - It is the first check constraint in the app. `stock_entry.origin` stays as it is, without one; this task doesn't touch it.
  - A schema test asserts the constraint's value list equals `{r.value for r in WithdrawalReason}`.
- **One place reads it.** `SqlStockWithdrawals._reason_of(row)` builds `WithdrawalReason(row.reason)`, and nothing else does. With the constraint in place, an unknown value can only come from someone bypassing the database's own check. I'm not adding a fallback that would hide it.
- **Where the mapping lives (adjustment).** `DrawSource` moves into its own module, `app/stock/domain/draw_source.py`, so that `withdrawal.py` and `lot_order.py` can both import it without a cycle. `domain/withdrawal.py` holds `draw_source_of(reason: WithdrawalReason) -> DrawSource`, an exhaustive `match` with `assert_never`.
- **Who calls it (adjustment).** Its one caller is `lots_and_draws`, when it turns a `WithdrawalLineDraw` (which carries `reason: WithdrawalReason`) into a `Draw`. It is not called in an adapter: the adapter only reads rows into `WithdrawalLineDraw`, so the reason-to-source decision stays in the domain and is unit-tested there.

**Q3**: **Accepted.**

- **One clock for both.** The router takes `TodayDep` (`get_today`, i.e. `business_day(clock())` in Argentina time) and the injected `Clock`, exactly as the stock entry routes do. `record_withdrawal` and `edit_withdrawal` get `today: date` for the future-date check and `now: datetime` for `recorded_at`.
- **`recorded_at` never changes on edit.** It is set on create and an edit doesn't touch it, as for stock entries.
- **Boundary test.** Through HTTP, with the movable clock at 2026-10-06T01:00Z (05/10 22:00 in Argentina):
  - a withdrawal dated 06/10 is refused with `stock.withdrawal_date_in_future`;
  - one dated 05/10 is recorded.
- **Tie order.** It is pinned only by pure domain tests on `lots_and_draws` and `allocate`. Their fixtures share the same `day` and `recorded_at` across a negative adjustment, a sale line and two withdrawal lines, and they assert the order adjustment → sale line → withdrawal lines by `position`, and which draw takes the lot's cost.
- **HTTP tests** assert only date and recording order.

**Q4**: **Accepted: split in two.**

- **4a, the withdrawal routes:**
  - `/withdrawals/new` (create; `?variety=` pre-chooses a variety) and `/withdrawals/:id` (detail, with edit by the same form, and delete);
  - the reason with no default, repeated varieties allowed, and the #80 inputs;
  - the `screens.json` entries;
  - the entries in Más (second) and Variedades;
  - the toasts and both "Eso ya no está." forms.
- **4b, the lines and the history:**
  - the Inicio lines and their notes;
  - the Análisis lines in the month header, the week and month rows and the since-first-record totals;
  - the variety page's "Sacar del stock" action and its movements list in "Stock y entradas" and "Ver todas". The page's action sits in 4b because it is the same screen file as the movements.
- **Order.** Both run in parallel after 2b and 3b.
- **Wire types.** They are written by hand, as today, in the existing shared modules in `web/src/api/`:
  - the withdrawal, its lines and the movement row in `stock.ts`;
  - `CostLineOut`'s twin, generalised from `PanelShortfall`, in `panel.ts`;
  - the Análisis fields in `analysis.ts`.

  No screen declares its own wire type.
- **What proves they match** is the mechanism already in the repo, `tests/test_contract_shapes.py`:
  - **Who adds the fixtures.** 2b adds `web/src/api/fixtures/withdrawal.json` and `movements.json`. 3b updates `panel.json`, `analysis.json` and `analysis-empty.json`.
  - **What the backend side checks.** Each backend test asserts its answer with `assert_matches_fixture`. `FIXTURE_ROUTES` and `tests/contract_nullable.py`'s `NULLABLE` gain the new routes and nullable fields (`promotion`, `own_use`, `note`), checked against `app.openapi()`.
  - **What the front side does.** The front fakes `fetch` with those same fixtures, typed by the shared modules, so `tsc` ties the types to the fixtures and pytest ties the fixtures to the API.

### On the invariants proposed
- **Replaced.** Every withdrawal write route goes through `stock_router` with `schedule_upkeep`, which rebuilds the whole business's catalog text. No use case reports varieties to upkeep.
- **Accepted, restated.** `reason` is stored only as a `WithdrawalReason` value, checked by `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason`, and read in one place. `draw_source_of` is called only from `lots_and_draws`.
- **Added.** The future-date check and `recorded_at` both come from the one injected clock, in the business's day.

### Closing

### Closing summary (round 4, the last)

**Decided**
- **Placement.** Withdrawals live in `app/stock/`, not in a new hexagon. ADR-0030 records it.
- **Draw model.**
  - `Draw` carries an explicit `source` (`DrawSource`, in its own module).
  - `WithdrawalLineDraw` has rank 2, and the tie order is adjustment, then sale line, then withdrawal line.
  - Every branch over `DrawSource` or `DrawKind` is an exhaustive `match` with `assert_never`.
  - `DrawKind` gains `promotion` and `own_use`.
- **Single assembly.** One `history()` function feeds `allocate` and `stock_level`, and `lots_and_draws` takes the withdrawal draws as a required argument.
- **Results.**
  - `Buckets` and `PeriodTotals` gain the two cost lines and their pending marks, and profit stays in the one formula.
  - Margins, best week and best weekday stay blind to withdrawals.
  - `scan` folds a withdrawal's day into `first_record`, with no new port.
- **Persistence.**
  - Two tables, `stock_withdrawal` and `stock_withdrawal_line`, both with `business_id`, in migration 0012.
  - A `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason` check constraint, with the reason stored as the enum's English value and read in one place.
  - Business-removal labels for both tables.
  - Line replacement runs a Core delete before the inserts.
  - Repeated varieties are allowed, with no merging.
  - Refusal codes in `contract.json`, registered together with the router.
- **Time.** The future-date check and `recorded_at` use one injected clock, in the business's day.
- **Upkeep.** The whole catalog text is rebuilt by `schedule_upkeep` on every withdrawal write, so no use case reports varieties.
- **Tests.**
  - Pure domain tests for the tie order.
  - HTTP tests through `TestClient`.
  - Your regression test: a withdrawal past stock leaves shortfall and cost of goods sold unchanged.
  - Agreement tests between `GET /panel` and `GET /analysis`.
  - The sale-detail test (`GET /sales/{id}`) with an earlier withdrawal.
  - The trigger-matrix tests.
  - `test_contract_shapes.py` with new fixtures.
- **Subtasks.**
  - 1a: the stock draw contract.
  - 1b: the aggregate and its errors, which can run in parallel with 1a.
  - 2a-i and 2a-ii: tables with migration and labels, then the repository, `history()` and the callers.
  - 3a: results domain.
  - 2b and 3b: the router and the answers.
  - 4a and 4b: the front, which waits on #59.
- **PM rulings.** A withdrawal counts as a first record. The glossary already says so.

**Known risks**
- Subtask 3a edits the 575-line `period_figures.py` (the new bucket fields, the `scan` branches and `first_record`). It is the piece most likely to hit the turn cap, and no split was agreed.
- The five existing `lots_and_draws` callers all change to `history()` at once in 2a-ii. If one is missed, the sale detail and results can disagree. The required argument and the sale-detail test are the only guards.
- Hand-written front wire types are tied to the API only by fixtures. A nullable field left out of `NULLABLE` would pass the front's `tsc` and fail only in the backend test.
- The `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason` constraint is the app's first check constraint. Its migration under SQLite batch mode is untested precedent.
- `stock_entry.origin` keeps no check constraint, so the two tables are now inconsistent.

## Tech Lead

### Approach

Stock withdrawals (Retiro: Promoción or Consumo propio) are a **third draw source inside `app/stock/`**, not a new hexagon (ADR-0030). A withdrawal's lines are draws in lot order, so they cost what `allocate` says. Results turns each draw into a `DrawRow` with a new `kind` (`promotion` or `own_use`). Those rows land in their own buckets of the one period computation (`app/results/domain/period_figures.py`), so Inicio and Análisis read the same figures, and profit stays a single formula. Sales, payments, margins, best week and best weekday never see a withdrawal.

### Decisions this plan builds to

- **Draw model (ADR-0030).**
  - `app/stock/domain/draw_source.py` holds three things:
    - `DrawSource` (`ADJUSTMENT`, `SALE_LINE`, `PROMOTION`, `OWN_USE`);
    - `WithdrawalReason` (`PROMOTION = "promotion"`, `OWN_USE = "own_use"`);
    - `draw_source_of(reason) -> DrawSource`, an exhaustive `match`.
  - **Change from the interview:** `WithdrawalReason` and `draw_source_of` sit in this module rather than in `withdrawal.py`. That lets the draw contract (1a) and the aggregate (1b) run in parallel without both editing one file.
  - `Draw.source` is required. `WithdrawalLineDraw` uses `WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK = 2`.
  - On an exact tie, the order is: adjustment, then sale line, then withdrawal line (by `position`).
- **One assembly.** `app/stock/application/history.py::history(entries, draws, withdrawal_draws, variety_ids)` is the only caller of `lots_and_draws`. It reads all three sources with the same `variety_ids`, where `None` means every variety. These five readers call `history` and nothing else:
  - `read_stock` and `read_variety_stock`;
  - `costs_for_sale` (`SqlLineCosts`);
  - `SqlDrawCosts.rows`;
  - `SqlStockFigures.read`.
- **Results.**
  - `build_draw_rows` becomes an exhaustive `match draw.source`.
  - `DrawKind` gains `promotion` and `own_use`. Their rows carry no variety, sale or line total.
  - `scan` matches on `row.kind`. Withdrawal rows feed only `Buckets.withdrawal_totals` and fold their day into `first_record`.
  - One `_cost_line` helper answers `shortfall`, `promotion` and `own_use`, each `AmountWithPending | None`.
  - `Buckets.profit` subtracts both costed amounts and adds both reasons' pending grams.
- **Persistence (migration `0012_stock_withdrawals`).** Two new tables; the subtask briefs have the full shapes.
  - `stock_withdrawal` stores the reason as English values, guarded by `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason`.
  - `stock_withdrawal_line` has `uq_..._stock_withdrawal_id_position`.
  - Both carry `business_id`.
  - An edit keeps the header's `id` and `recorded_at`. It replaces the lines with a Core delete before any insert.
- **API, on `stock_router`, so upkeep runs after commit with no new wiring (ADR-0014):**
  - `POST /stock/withdrawals` (201);
  - `GET /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`;
  - `PUT /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`;
  - `DELETE /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}` (204);
  - `GET /stock/varieties/{variety_id}/movements`.
- **Front routes:** `/withdrawals/new` (optionally `?variety=<id>`) and `/withdrawals/:id`. `withdrawals` is not an API first segment, so ADR-0024 does not apply. It joins `contract.json`'s `frontSegments`.
- **Time.** Both the future-date check and `recorded_at` come from the one injected clock (`TodayDep` and `Clock`), in Argentina time.

### What it widens (§8)

- A signed-in user can now record, read, edit and delete withdrawals of their own business only. The business comes from the session.
- A line's variety must exist in that business; otherwise the request answers 404.
- Nothing crosses businesses. Every new route joins the isolation sweep and the gated-route test.

### Cost (§8)

- The whole-history replay is unchanged in shape. `history()` adds one query for withdrawal lines, so a read costs O(entries + sale lines + withdrawal lines) of the business (or of the narrowed varieties).
- `GET /panel` and `GET /analysis` each still run a fixed number of statements, one more than today. The existing tests that compare statement counts on a small and a big seeded business must keep passing with the new constant.
- `GET /stock/varieties/{id}/movements` costs O(entries + withdrawals) of that variety.

### Observability and privacy (§7, §9)

- Nothing new is emitted.
- Refusals carry fixed messages and codes. No note, grams or amount appears in an error, a log, the console or browser storage.

### Coexistence with old shapes

- `stock_entry.entry_date` keeps its allowlisted name next to the new `withdrawn_on`.
- `stock_entry.origin` keeps no check constraint. `ck_stock_withdrawal_reason` is the app's first check constraint, created inside `op.create_table`, not in batch mode.
- `_ShortfallDayTotals` is generalised into `_CostDayTotals`, not duplicated.
- On the panel, `ShortfallOut` is generalised into `CostLineOut`. The `shortfall` key and its shape are unchanged.

### Known risks

- **`period_figures.py` (575 lines).** The results domain is split into two sequential subtasks (`results-buckets`, then `results-windows`) to stay under the turn cap.
- **The five `lots_and_draws` callers move to `history()` together, in `stock-history`.** Two things guard it:
  - the required third argument, which makes `mypy` fail a caller that forgets it;
  - the test in `stock-withdrawal-api` that reads the same sale line through `GET /sales/{id}` and `GET /analysis` and finds the same cost.
- **Hand-written front wire types** are tied to the API only by fixtures and `tests/contract_nullable.py`. A nullable field missing there is caught only in the backend test.
- **No design files yet.** The withdrawal form and its detail have no `.dc.html`. The screen names below are this plan's. If design delivers other names, design's names win, and `screens.json` follows them.

### Subtasks

`### Subtasks` written by the system from the structured plan: no exact `### Subtasks` line (outside code fences) was found in this section, so the block below was built from the structured plan. Any list the Tech Lead wrote under another heading is not the one the Developers read.

#### Stock domain: DrawSource, withdrawal draws in lot order, days left

Build the draw contract recorded in docs/adr/0030 (do not rewrite the ADR; match it).

Files: new `app/stock/domain/draw_source.py`; `app/stock/domain/lot_order.py`; `app/stock/domain/stock_level.py`; `app/results/domain/ledger.py`; the five current callers of `lots_and_draws` (only to keep them compiling).

- `draw_source.py`: `DrawSource` enum (`ADJUSTMENT`, `SALE_LINE`, `PROMOTION`, `OWN_USE`); `WithdrawalReason` enum with values `"promotion"` and `"own_use"`; `draw_source_of(reason: WithdrawalReason) -> DrawSource`, an exhaustive `match` ending in `assert_never`.
- `lot_order.py`:
  - `Draw` gains a required `source: DrawSource`. Negative adjustments get `ADJUSTMENT`, sale lines `SALE_LINE`.
  - Add `WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK = 2`. `STOCK_ENTRY_RANK = 0` and `SALE_LINE_RANK = 1` keep their values.
  - Add the frozen dataclass `WithdrawalLineDraw(variety_id, day, recorded_at, withdrawal_id, position, grams, reason: WithdrawalReason)`.
  - `lots_and_draws(entries, sale_draws, withdrawal_draws)` takes the third argument with no default. It turns each withdrawal line into `Draw(LotOrder(day, recorded_at, WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK, withdrawal_id, position), variety_id, grams, source=draw_source_of(reason))`. Note that `draw_source_of` is called only here.
  - Update the module docstring.
- `stock_level._days_left`: count draws through an exhaustive `match draw.source`. `SALE_LINE`, `PROMOTION` and `OWN_USE` count; `ADJUSTMENT` does not. Read nothing from `rank` any more.
- `results/domain/ledger.py`: `DrawKind = Literal["sold", "gifted", "shortfall", "promotion", "own_use"]`. Change nothing else in results; `results-buckets` owns the rest.
- The five callers (`read_stock` ×2, `line_costs.costs_for_sale`, `SqlDrawCosts.rows`, `SqlStockFigures.read`) pass `withdrawal_draws=[]` for now. `stock-history` replaces them. Do not touch `draw_rows.py`.

Tests (pure domain, no infrastructure):
- An exact tie of `day` and `recorded_at` across a negative adjustment, a sale line and two withdrawal lines orders them adjustment → sale line → withdrawal lines by position. `allocate` gives the first lot's cost to the expected draw.
- A withdrawal past every lot is pending grams, never costed $0. A variety's total pending equals `max(0, −stock)` with a withdrawal involved.
- `stock_level`:
  - withdrawn grams pace days left; an adjustment does not;
  - a variety with only a withdrawal is sold out, not "no entries";
  - sold-out-since follows a withdrawal's date.
- `draw_source_of` maps both reasons.

Acceptance: `mypy --strict` and `ruff` clean; existing stock and results tests pass unchanged.

Seams: `app.stock.domain.lot_order.lots_and_draws`, `app.stock.domain.allocation.allocate`, `app.stock.domain.stock_level.stock_level`, `app.stock.domain.draw_source.draw_source_of`.

Depends on: nothing.

#### Stock domain: the Withdrawal aggregate and its refusals

New `app/stock/domain/withdrawal.py`; new error classes in `app/stock/domain/errors.py` (or `withdrawal.py`, as long as stock's error module stays one place to look).

- `WithdrawalLine` value object: `variety_id: int`, `grams: Grams`. Grams must be above zero (`WithdrawalGramsNotPositive`). More than two decimals is `WithdrawalGramsTooPrecise`: how a raw grams input becomes `Grams` must refuse it with this class. Check how `Grams` parsing works today and raise the withdrawal's own class.
- `Withdrawal` aggregate:
  - `id: int | None`, `reason: WithdrawalReason` (import it from `app/stock/domain/draw_source.py`, which `stock-draw-contract` creates; if that subtask has not merged yet, create the enum there with exactly the values `promotion` and `own_use`);
  - `withdrawn_on: date`, `note: str | None`, `lines: tuple[WithdrawalLine, ...]`, `recorded_at: datetime`.
  - Its constructor (or a `record(...)` factory taking `today`) enforces:
    - a reason is required: a missing or unknown raw value is `WithdrawalReasonRequired`;
    - a date after `today` is `WithdrawalDateInFuture`;
    - no lines is `WithdrawalNoLines`;
    - the note is trimmed, blank means `None`, and over 200 characters is `WithdrawalNoteTooLong` (reuse `NOTE_MAX_LENGTH` from `entry.py` if it is 200).
  - The same variety may appear in several lines, with no merging.
  - An edit builds a new `Withdrawal` with the same validation, keeping `id` and `recorded_at`.
  - `total_grams` sums the lines (used by the front's delete confirmation through the API).
- Errors: `WithdrawalNotFound` and `WithdrawalVarietyNotFound` are `LookupError`s (kind gone). Every class carries `code: ClassVar[str]` with these codes:
  - `stock.withdrawal_reason_required`
  - `stock.withdrawal_date_in_future`
  - `stock.withdrawal_no_lines`
  - `stock.withdrawal_grams_not_positive`
  - `stock.withdrawal_grams_too_precise`
  - `stock.withdrawal_note_too_long`
  - `stock.withdrawal_not_found`
  - `stock.withdrawal_variety_not_found`

  Every message is fixed: no note, grams or ids.
- Do NOT add the codes to `web/src/api/contract.json` here. `stock-withdrawal-api` adds them in the same diff that registers the classes on the router, since `tests/test_refusal_codes.py` checks registered classes against the JSON.

Tests: domain unit tests for every refusal and for the accepted edge cases (today's date, exactly 200 characters, repeated variety, 0.01 g). Assert that no message contains the input.

Seams: `app.stock.domain.withdrawal.Withdrawal`.

Depends on: nothing.

#### Tables, migration 0012 and business-removal labels for withdrawals

Files: `app/stock/adapters/tables.py`; new `app/migrations/versions/0012_stock_withdrawals.py` (revises 0011); `app/business_removal/domain/labels.py`; `app/tables.py` / `TABLE_MODULES` only if a new module is added (prefer keeping the classes in stock's `tables.py`).

- `StockWithdrawalLineRow` (`stock_withdrawal_line`):

- Business removal's own migrated-schema twin tests pass: no unlabelled table, no secret or OTHER-typed column.

#### Results: draw rows by source, withdrawal buckets, profit and first record

Files: `app/stock/adapters/draw_rows.py`; `app/results/domain/period_figures.py` (the `Buckets`/`scan`/`totals_between` part only); `app/results/domain/figures.py` (only `PeriodTotals` and `AmountWithPending`, if a helper is needed).

- `build_draw_rows`: replace the nested ternary with `match draw.source`, ending in `assert_never`:
  - `SALE_LINE` → `gifted` when the stored pricing rule is gift, else `sold`, with variety, sale and line total as today;
  - `ADJUSTMENT` → `shortfall`;
  - `PROMOTION` → `promotion`;
  - `OWN_USE` → `own_use`.

  The last three carry `variety_id`, `variety_name`, `sale_id` and `line_total` as `None`. The kind is never told by a missing field.
- `period_figures.py`:
  - Rename `_ShortfallDayTotals` to `_CostDayTotals(costed, pending_grams)`.
  - `Buckets` gains `withdrawal_totals: dict[tuple[date, Literal["promotion","own_use"]], _CostDayTotals]`.
  - One private `_cost_line(...) -> AmountWithPending | None` answers `shortfall`, `promotion` and `own_use`, each `None` exactly when no draw of that kind falls in the window.
  - `scan` loops over draws with `match row.kind` ending in `assert_never`:
    - `shortfall` as today;
    - `promotion` and `own_use` write only `withdrawal_totals` and fold `row.day` into `first_record`;
    - `sold` and `gifted` as today.

    Withdrawal rows never touch `day_totals`, `variety_day_totals`, `sales_by_day` or the weekday buckets.
  - `Buckets.profit` stays the one formula. It subtracts the costed promotion and own-use amounts and adds both reasons' pending grams to profit's pending.
  - `PeriodTotals` gains `promotion` and `own_use` (each `AmountWithPending | None`), filled by `totals_between`.
  - Do NOT change the month, week, months and since-first-record builders; `results-windows` does those.

Tests (domain, over `DrawRow` fixtures and `build_draw_rows` over hand-built lots and draws):
- each source maps to its kind;
- a promotion and an own-use row each lower profit by their costed amount on their own date;
- `shortfall` and cost of goods are unchanged by a withdrawal, as are sales, grams sold or gifted, and weekday sales;
- a pending-only withdrawal gives profit a pending count and leaves cost of goods with no pending;
- each reason's pending counts only its own grams, and profit adds all (the PRD's 4 g + 2 g + 1 g = 7 g example);
- a withdrawal dated before every other record moves `first_record`;
- a window with no withdrawal of a reason answers `None` for it.

Seams: `app.stock.adapters.draw_rows.build_draw_rows`, `app.results.domain.period_figures.scan`, `app.results.domain.period_figures.totals_between`.

Depends on: `stock-draw-contract`.

#### Withdrawal repository, history() and the five readers

Files:
- new `app/stock/adapters/sql_stock_withdrawals.py`;
- `app/stock/application/ports.py` (new `Withdrawals` and `WithdrawalDraws` protocols);
- new `app/stock/application/history.py`;
- `read_stock.py`, `line_costs.py`, `draw_costs.py`, `stock_figures.py`.

- `SqlStockWithdrawals(session, business_id)` implements both ports. Every statement is filtered on `business_id`, the `ON` clauses of any join included.
  - `by_id(id) -> Withdrawal | None`: another business's id answers `None`.
  - `add(withdrawal) -> Withdrawal`: header, then lines with positions `0..n-1` in the given order.
  - `replace(withdrawal) -> Withdrawal`:
    - update the header; `id` and `recorded_at` never change;
    - run a Core `sa_delete(StockWithdrawalLineRow).where(business_id, stock_withdrawal_id)` through `session.execute` before adding any line;
    - then insert the new lines and flush.
  - `remove(id)`: lines then header, both Core deletes.
  - `for_varieties(variety_ids | None) -> list[WithdrawalLineDraw]`, plus a read for the movements list that `stock-withdrawal-api` needs: the withdrawals touching one variety, with that variety's summed grams per withdrawal.
  - `SqlStockWithdrawals._reason_of(row)` is the only place `WithdrawalReason(row.reason)` is built. It has no fallback.
  - Repositories flush and never commit.
- `history(entries, draws, withdrawal_draws, variety_ids: Collection[int] | None)` reads the three sources with the same `variety_ids` and returns `lots_and_draws(...)`. It is the only caller of `lots_and_draws` in the codebase. The five readers call `history` and no longer call `lots_and_draws` or a source's `for_varieties` themselves.

Tests, at the repository and `history` seams over a fresh in-memory SQLite with the real adapters:
- add, then read back with lines in order;
- a replace with the same number of lines, and one that reverses their order, never trips the unique constraint;
- a foreign business's withdrawal is invisible to `by_id` and `for_varieties`;
- `history` narrowed to one variety and un-narrowed give that variety's draws in the same order and cost.

HTTP behaviour tests come in `stock-withdrawal-api`.

Seams: `app.stock.adapters.sql_stock_withdrawals.SqlStockWithdrawals`, `app.stock.application.history.history`.

Depends on: `stock-draw-contract`, `withdrawal-tables`, `results-buckets`.

#### Withdrawal use cases, routes, refusal codes and HTTP behaviour

Files:
- new `app/stock/application/manage_withdrawals.py`;
- `app/stock/adapters/router.py` and `schemas.py`;
- `web/src/api/contract.json` (refusal codes only);
- new fixtures `web/src/api/fixtures/withdrawal.json` and `movements.json`;
- `tests/contract_nullable.py`, plus `FIXTURE_ROUTES` in `tests/test_contract_shapes.py`;
- `tests/test_business_isolation.py` (seeder and `BODIES`).

- Use cases: `record_withdrawal`, `edit_withdrawal`, `delete_withdrawal`, `read_withdrawal`, `read_movements`.
  - Each one that writes commits once, at its end, through `Transaction`.
  - Each line's variety is checked through `VarietyCatalog.exists`; an unknown one raises `WithdrawalVarietyNotFound`.
  - `today` and `now` come from the router's `TodayDep` and `Clock`, as the stock entry routes do. `recorded_at` is set only on record.
  - A withdrawal is never refused for lack of stock or for grams already drawn.
- Routes on `stock_router`, which is already included with `upkeep_gated`:
  - `POST /stock/withdrawals` → 201 with the withdrawal;
  - `GET /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`;
  - `PUT /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`, with the whole withdrawal in the body;
  - `DELETE /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}` → 204;
  - `GET /stock/varieties/{variety_id}/movements`.
- Schemas stay bare (ADR-0005).
  - Body: `reason: str | None`, `date`, `note: str | None`, `lines: [{variety_id, grams}]`, with grams in the same input shape stock entries use.
  - Answer: `{id, reason, date, note, total_grams, lines: [{variety_id, variety_name, grams}]}`.
  - Movements: a list, newest first, ordered on the server by (day desc, recorded_at desc, kind, id desc). Each item is `{kind: "entry"|"withdrawal", id, date, ...}`. Entries carry the fields their history row needs today: origin, grams, cost. Withdrawals carry `reason` and that variety's summed grams in it. One row per withdrawal, even with repeated lines.
- `TranslateErrors` registers the eight withdrawal classes (404 for the two gone ones). Add the eight codes to `contract.json` in this same diff.

HTTP tests (`TestClient`, `client`/`other_client`, movable clock):
- the validation matrix, one coded refusal per case, and no body containing the note, grams or amounts;
- the clock at 2026-10-06T01:00Z refuses a withdrawal dated 06/10 and accepts 05/10;
- record, edit and delete move `GET /stock` exactly (in minus out, to the hundredth); a withdrawal past stock is recorded and shows sold out;
- days left, "no entries" and sold-out-since behave as stock.md says;
- an edit with the same line count, and one with reversed lines, answers 200;
- `GET /sales/{id}`: a line's cost moves when an earlier-dated withdrawal on its variety is recorded, edited and deleted, and equals the same line's cost seen through `GET /analysis`'s month (the five readers agree);
- after a withdrawal: sales list, sales totals and count, customer balances and account, and Caja are unchanged;
- movements mix entries and withdrawals in order, with a withdrawal touching two varieties shown on each;
- pinned-catalog trigger matrix, with the fake Telegram adapter:
  - an edit moving the only line from A to B edits the pin once, and the text lacks A;
  - a delete that brings a variety back above zero edits it;
  - a note-only edit sends nothing;
  - every `/stock/withdrawals` write route carries `schedule_upkeep`;
- isolation sweep: seed a withdrawal on the `MARKER` variety; every new route answers 404 or empty from the other business, including a body naming the other business's variety;
- gated routes;
- business removal's "nothing survives" and export = delete, seeded with a two-line withdrawal;
- answers match the new fixtures through `assert_matches_fixture`.

Seams: `POST /stock/withdrawals`, `GET /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`, `PUT /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`, `DELETE /stock/withdrawals/{withdrawal_id}`, `GET /stock/varieties/{variety_id}/movements`, `GET /stock`, `GET /sales/{sale_id}`.

#### Front: Sacar del stock form, withdrawal detail, Más and Variedades entries

Waits for #59 on master (the navigation shell).

Files:
- `web/src/api/stock.ts`: the withdrawal, line and movement wire types, hand-written to match `withdrawal.json` and `movements.json`; the movement type is shared with `front-results-and-history`;
- new screens for the form and the detail;
- `App.tsx` routes;
- Más and Variedades (phone and computer);
- `contract.json` (`frontSegments` gains `withdrawals`) and `web/src/api/refusals.ts` (Spanish copy for the eight codes);
- `scripts/design/screens.json` entries.

- **`/withdrawals/new`**, the form "Sacar del stock". It is a form under the navigation rule: "Cerrar", no bar, no "+ Nueva venta", and the leave guard (`useLeaveGuard`) when something is typed.
  - Reason: Promoción · Consumo propio, required, no default.
  - Date: today, never later.
  - One or more lines of variety and grams, repeated varieties allowed, with "Agregar otra variedad".
  - Note.
  - `?variety=<id>` pre-chooses the first line's variety.
  - #80 phone standard: 44 px targets, inputs at least 16 px, grams `inputmode="decimal"`, `enterkeyhint` through a line.
  - Any sheet inside it (picking a variety) uses `useSheet` (ADR-0027).
  - Save shows the toast "Retiro guardado" and goes back by history.
  - Refusals show inline and keep what was typed.
- **`/withdrawals/:id`**, the detail. It shows the bar or sidebar and a "‹" back to the variety's page it came from, or Variedades with no history, and no "+ Nueva venta".
  - It shows the date, the reason by word, every line and the note.
  - "Editar" opens the same form filled in. Saving an edit is confirmed first.
  - "Borrar" asks "¿Borrás la promoción del dd/mm? Vuelven N g al stock." or "¿Borrás el consumo propio del dd/mm? …", with "No, dejalo / Sí, borralo". Then the toast "Retiro eliminado".
- Gone (404):
  - opening the detail shows "Eso ya no está." with "Volver a Variedades";
  - saving, deleting or confirming on a gone withdrawal shows "Eso ya no está. La lista ya está al día.".
- Más: "Sacar del stock" second, right after "+ Cargar gasto". Variedades (phone and computer): a secondary "Sacar del stock" next to "+ Entrada de stock". Both open the form with no variety chosen.
- Formats come from the shared format module. Nothing goes to the console or storage.

- the reason is required;
- the toasts;
- edit and delete confirmations with their fixed text;
- both gone forms;
- the Más order;
- the Variedades button;
- a refusal keeps the fields.

Look at the design comparisons before delivering.

### Changes
- `app/stock/domain/draw_source.py` (new): `DrawSource` (`ADJUSTMENT`, `SALE_LINE`, `PROMOTION`, `OWN_USE`), `WithdrawalReason` (`"promotion"`, `"own_use"`), and `draw_source_of`, an exhaustive `match` ending in `assert_never`.
- `app/stock/domain/lot_order.py`:
  - `Draw.source` is required.
  - Added `WITHDRAWAL_LINE_RANK = 2`; `STOCK_ENTRY_RANK` and `SALE_LINE_RANK` keep their values.
  - Added `WithdrawalLineDraw`.
  - `lots_and_draws(entries, sale_draws, withdrawal_draws)` has no default for the third argument. It builds each withdrawal line as `Draw(LotOrder(day, recorded_at, 2, withdrawal_id, position), …, draw_source_of(reason))`. `draw_source_of` is called only here.
  - Negative adjustments get `ADJUSTMENT`, sale lines `SALE_LINE`. Docstrings updated.
- `app/stock/domain/stock_level.py`: `_days_left` uses `_paces(draw)`, an exhaustive `match draw.source`. Sale lines and both withdrawal sources count; adjustments do not. It no longer reads `rank`.
- `app/results/domain/ledger.py`: `DrawKind` gains `promotion` and `own_use`.
- Five callers (`read_stock` ×2, `costs_for_sale`, `SqlDrawCosts.rows`, `SqlStockFigures.read`): pass `withdrawal_draws=[]` so they keep compiling.

### Tests
- `tests/stock/test_draw_source.py`: `draw_source_of` maps both reasons; stored values are the English words.
- `tests/stock/test_lot_order.py`, at `lots_and_draws` and `allocate`:
  - An exact tie orders adjustment, then sale line, then withdrawal lines by position. The ids and the input order are set so only `rank` and `position` produce that order, and `allocate` gives the lot's cost to the expected draws.
  - A withdrawal past every lot is pending, never costed $0.
  - Total pending equals `max(0, −stock)` with a withdrawal involved.
  - A withdrawal draw carries no sale data.
- `tests/stock/test_stock_level.py`, at `stock_level`:
  - Both withdrawal sources pace days left; an adjustment does not.
  - A variety with only a withdrawal is sold out, not "no entries".
  - Sold-out-since follows the withdrawal's date.
- Existing tests only needed the new `source` and `withdrawal_draws` arguments.

### Decisions and assumptions
- `Draw.source` is placed before the defaulted sale fields.
- A wholly pending draw has `costed=None`, not 0 (existing `allocate` behaviour), so the tests assert `None`.
- `ruff format --check` is not a declared check and fails on 71 files already, so I left formatting alone.

### For the Tech Lead
Look first at `lots_and_draws` and `WithdrawalLineDraw` in `app/stock/domain/lot_order.py`, then `_paces` in `stock_level.py`. The five callers pass `withdrawal_draws=[]` and `stock-history` replaces them with `history()`. The existing `draw_rows.py` still tells a draw's kind by a missing `sale_id`, as the brief says; `results-buckets` must make it an exhaustive `match draw.source`.

- `app/stock/domain/errors.py`: eight new refusals, each with its own `code`.
  - Six are `ValueError`s: reason required, date in future, no lines, grams not positive, grams too precise, note too long.
  - Two are `LookupError`s with no-argument fixed messages: `WithdrawalNotFound` and `WithdrawalVarietyNotFound`.
- `app/stock/domain/withdrawal.py` (new): `WithdrawalLine` and `Withdrawal`.
  - `WithdrawalLine` is a frozen value object that validates grams above zero in `__post_init__`. Its `.of(variety_id, raw)` parses raw grams and refuses more than two decimals with the withdrawal's own class.
  - `Withdrawal.of` is the one constructor for recording and editing. It checks reason, date, lines and note (trimmed, blank means `None`, at most `NOTE_MAX_LENGTH`) in that order. `total_grams` sums the lines.

### Tests
`tests/stock/test_withdrawal.py`, at the seam `app.stock.domain.withdrawal.Withdrawal`, domain only with no infrastructure.
- Accepted cases: dated today, an earlier date, a 200-character note, a padded note trimmed before it is measured, a blank note becoming `None`, 0.01 g, repeated varieties kept unmerged, and an edit keeping `id` and `recorded_at`.
- Refusal cases: missing or unknown reason, future date, no lines, zero/negative/non-numeric/NaN grams, more than two decimals, a note over 200 characters, and the same validation on an edit.
- Codes: every refusal carries its code.
- No echo: refusal messages contain none of the input, and the not-found messages carry no digits.

### Decisions and assumptions
- Non-numeric grams map to `WithdrawalGramsNotPositive`, because the brief lists no separate code.
- `Grams.of` is bypassed in `WithdrawalLine.of` so the too-precise case raises the withdrawal's own class. `HUNDREDTHS_PER_GRAM` is imported from `app.shared.grams`.
- A `None` reason is turned into `""` before `WithdrawalReason(...)`, so it raises the same refusal as an unknown value.
- Invariant 13: the codes are declared on the classes here, and `contract.json` entries are left to `stock-withdrawal-api` per the brief.

### For the Tech Lead
- `draw_source.py` will conflict with `stock-draw-contract` if both create it. Mine is a minimal file holding only the enum, so resolving it is trivial.
- No codes were added to `contract.json`; `stock-withdrawal-api` does that when it registers the classes.
- The first presubmit failed `web-tests` (four files: timeouts at 5000 ms, plus a Expenses test) and `web-e2e` (`ventasTelefono.spec.ts`, a strict-mode locator hitting two customers). This diff changes no `web/` file. `NuevaVenta.linea.test.tsx` passes when run alone (5 of 5). I did not re-run the other three failing test files or the e2e, so I can't say they are flaky.
- `ruff format --check .` reports 71 files that need reformatting across the repo; format is not one of the declared checks. The files I touched are formatted.
